Player_White (to Proxy):

The position on the board offers a fantastic tactical opportunity to exploit the awkward placement of Black's dark-squared bishop on **a5**. In typical Ragozin or QGD setups, Black has a d-pawn to react to queenside pawn pushes. However, in this unique pawn structure where Black's d-pawn is completely absent, Black's bishop on a5 is highly vulnerable to being trapped.

By playing **1. b4**, we force Black into an extremely uncomfortable situation:
1. If Black retreats with **1... Bb6**, we follow up with **2. c5!**, completely trapping the bishop since Black cannot play ...dxc5 to free it.
2. If Black retreats with **1... Ba6**, we play the fork **2. b5!**, winning a minor piece.
3. If Black tries **1... Nxb4 2. axb4 Bxb4**, Black gives up a whole minor piece for just two pawns, giving White a decisive material advantage.
4. If Black plays **1... Bxc3+ 2. bxc3**, Black surrenders their active bishop, giving us the bishop pair, absolute control over the center, and a highly favorable pawn structure.

Thus, **b2b4** is a direct and winning move.
